# Test catalog

Question bank backend additions:

- `server/internal/bank/bank_test.go`: independent migration ledger, syllabus/list queries, optimistic saves, review retained on edit, review undo, editor column grants, missing config and unreachable pool.
- `server/internal/cover/cover_test.go`: `Cover.Check` accepts each style's own fields, a seed and the default cover, and refuses an upper-case colour, a missing or stray kind, an unknown pattern, a line outside paper, a bad seed and an unknown style.
- `server/internal/bank/assets_test.go`: immutable content-hash publication metadata and rejection of active, external-resource and oversized SVG assets.
- `server/internal/bank/page_test.go`: the topic pager (`PageRows`) pages forward and back by position cursor, `around` lands on the page holding a question, a row that stops matching between pages (answered under the Status filter) shifts nothing, the type, status, search and unreviewed filters, the topic's answer types, and a malformed cursor refused.
- `server/internal/bank/progress_test.go`: the /qb landing's topic progress counts only current answered questions (an edited or retracted question is unanswered, a topic with none is left out), counts full-mark scores as correct, puts the latest answered topic first, and points Continue at the next unanswered question after the latest answer, wrapping to the start, or nowhere once every question is answered.
- `server/internal/httpapi/bank_test.go`: batch reads in request order with attribution and 404 for an unknown id, answer-free single and batch reads for learners and for editors in View mode, the editor-only `/edit` read with the keys (404 for a learner), editor/read-only/suspended gates, stale saves, public SVG upload, historical source attribution with ShareAlike licensing, web-page attribution and direct captured comment email.
- `scripts/env/test_config.py`: bank owner/private-bucket secrets excluded from the API target and optional bank configuration.

One-line descriptions of every automated test file in the repo (excluding build
artifacts under `collaboration/dist` and vendored packages). Run commands are
at the top of each section.

Responsive styling changes also need a manual viewport check below and above
`sm` (640px): IconPicker uses three/six columns and 350px/440px gallery heights;
SectionGallery spacing and toast action sizing increase at `sm`; workspace
labels, note AI suggestions and editor padding use small-screen base styles.
These visual checks are not covered by the automated suites below.

Chat history styling is checked manually in the MSW Biology workspace: open
Chat history, inspect SimpleDialog spacing and list scrolling, select a saved
conversation, reopen to check its active marker, and dismiss with Escape.

| Suite                            | Command                                                          |
| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Frontend Vitest                  | `pnpm test`                                                      |
| Ops dashboard Vitest             | `pnpm --filter @capy-notebook/ops test`                              |
| Collaboration Vitest             | `pnpm test:collaboration`                                        |
| Go server                        | `pnpm test:go`                                                   |
| Python pipeline                  | `pnpm test:pipeline` / offline: `pnpm test:pipeline:offline`     |
| Certified model cassette replay  | `pnpm test:pipeline:replay`                                      |
| Playwright sharing/API e2e       | `pnpm e2e` / serialized: `pnpm e2e:slow`                         |
| Playwright editor (MSW)          | `pnpm e2e:msw:editor`                                            |
| Playwright editor perf           | `pnpm bench:editor`                                              |
| Playwright authorized UAT        | `pnpm e2e:uat`                                                   |
| Office storage capacity probes | Live UAT measurement via `pnpm exec playwright test --config bench/parsers/scripts/office_storage_ingest.config.ts`. `bench/parsers/scripts/compare_office_storage.py` verifies paired input hashes, complete phase triples, unchanged ingest images, clean teardown and cache removal before assembling exact byte tables. (The engine probe `office_storage.ts` and the charging run were retired with the Node collaboration server, Epo 2026-10-08.) |
| Review automation contracts      | `pnpm review:validate-boundaries` / `pnpm review:validate-scanners` |
| Deployment and dev tunnel        | `pnpm test:deployment` |
| Local grading comparison         | `python -m unittest discover -s bench/grading/scripts -p test_benchmark.py` |
| Paired Laya/Jev grading scores    | `python -m unittest discover -s bench/grading/scripts -p test_laya_cpu.py` |
| Office size split (XML/media/other) | `python -m unittest discover -s bench/editor/scripts -p test_office_sizes.py` |
| Office parity checklists (CI `office_pin`) | `python3 bench/parity/scripts/parity.py --check`; the generator's own tests `python3 -m unittest discover -s bench/parity/scripts -p test_parity.py` cover Rust and TS test-name scanning (attributes, multi-line titles, `test.each` tables, computed titles skipped), evidence resolution, a Y without a test for its aspect, malformed rows, priority-weighted parity, the benchmark summary, exit 1 with nothing written, and `--check` failing on a stale or missing report |
| Jev question-context comparison | `python bench/grading/scripts/jev_context.py --check` |
| Jev partial credit and context | `python bench/grading/scripts/jev_partial_credit.py --check` |
| Jev production contract | `python bench/grading/scripts/jev_contract.py --check` |
| Alternative-method grading | `python bench/grading/scripts/alt_methods.py --check` |
| Browser grading failure lifecycle | `node bench/grading/scripts/test_browser.mjs` |
| Agent playground self-checks     | `python lab/playground/scripts/{capture.py, chunk_quality.py --check, playground.py --check}`; `node lab/playground/scripts/check_ui.mjs` checks prompt Apply/Reset, draft preservation across delayed previews, follow-up evidence payloads, saved-run history/checkpoint restoration without config changes, material-dialog content for restored notes and live quizzes/flashcards, the output-preview frame for a restored material (raw text open when no run is known), and conversation clearing. Python checks include description overrides, unchanged argument schemas, live catalog preservation, invalid override rejection, missing model pins returning HTTP errors before streaming, one capture_page schema in previews and live turns, local source-list dispatch without a gateway, forwarding evidence and checkpoints through the turn endpoint (the run recording its library reads), the output preview's deck slides with figures inlined and its `/embed/` frame carrying the CSP line of `embed/_headers`, note fence checks (flashcards, html-embed html without a fallback, size refusals, a CDN script and the SVG namespace accepted, another origin, a lookalike host and `fetch` refused, YAML errors), and the playground-only deck tools (`deck.py`, ppt-master's Quick route; clones the pinned ppt-master into `local/` on first run): the outline returning the style and its reference slides, slides refused for unparseable XML, another canvas, no `lang`, text outside its module (ppt-master's checker), an uncaptured or whole-page figure, or an out-of-range number, an accepted slide with a cropped capture, the `.pptx` exported once every slide is written, and schema validation. The question tools through the run's files: a new quiz saving its valid question and naming the other (a unit with a text answer), the fix (an answer written with its unit), an open question and a plot after the first question's text, each saved quiz passing `quizcheck`, a note's quiz fence expanded from the small shapes with its trailing comma repaired and named, and a later turn adding to the quiz an earlier turn made. Local note writes placing a library figure keep its render under the run's `assets/`, record it on the material for the preview with the printed credit in the caption and the figure's excerpt credited, an edit adds its own, and `add_question_media` places one after a quiz question's text as an image block keeping its `figure:<id>` asset with its render recorded on the quiz. The local `inspect_document`/`read_document`/`edit_document` on a run's note and card set: block ids given once and kept across edits and turns, an insert, block replacement and text replacement landing where their ids say, a stale expected text refusing the call, and a card added after another. `library_flow`: `browse_first` leaving browse a subject only, `search_subject` moving the subject list into search and dropping browse. `skill_addons` appended to a skill's text; `preference_text` replacing one preference value's turn-context line for a turn only and refusing a name outside `preferences.py`. |

Playwright's sharing/API global setup builds the collaboration Docker image,
including Office WASM with Binaryen's version and verified archive checksum from
the pinned BetterOffice checkout. On ARM hosts, run
`DOCKER_DEFAULT_PLATFORM=linux/amd64 pnpm e2e:slow` for its x86_64 Linux toolchain.

The frontend and browser E2E CI jobs reuse compiled browser WASM through
`.github/actions/cache-betteroffice`; normal `office:prepare` source and output
hash validation still runs before consuming the restored artifacts. This cache
does not cover the separate collaboration Docker build.

[`scripts/dev/test_tunnel.py`](../scripts/dev/test_tunnel.py) checks webhook-only routing
and development-key enforcement with a fake Cloudflare CLI.
[`scripts/dev/hosts.test.mjs`](../scripts/dev/hosts.test.mjs) checks macOS/Windows
elevation command construction and argument quoting, hosts paths, idempotent
edits, backup preservation, line endings, aliases and conflicting mappings.

The local grading checks in [`bench/grading/scripts/test_benchmark.py`](../bench/grading/scripts/test_benchmark.py) cover invalid-score handling, family split integrity, interrupted result recovery, separation of run configurations and changed coverage, exclusion of anchors/ambiguous labels from primary matched/native metrics, complete hash-matched comparison with invalid paired scores, and native/browser pairing restricted to identical cases, model hashes and explicit decoding controls. [`test_browser.mjs`](../bench/grading/scripts/test_browser.mjs) checks that a runtime exception saves bounded diagnostic logs, stops requests to the failed worker, releases it, permits the next model to run and visibly reports the failure. Model evaluations are opt-in experiments described in [`bench/grading/README.md`](../bench/grading/README.md), with artifacts under ignored `data/grading-benchmark/`.

[`test_laya_cpu.py`](../bench/grading/scripts/test_laya_cpu.py) checks that partial-award agreement does not hide marking-point errors, and that an inference failure counts as a failure while preserving the archived Jev baseline. `laya_cpu.py prepare` also verifies the archived 1,688-request rubric corpus reproduces Jev's published seed and essay award counts before writing the replay requests.
The opt-in [`alibaba_decision.py`](../bench/grading/scripts/alibaba_decision.py) replay uses the same scoring checks, validates returned question ids and finite probabilities, and records hosted API failures without retries. It requires explicitly selected credentials and an Alibaba workspace endpoint; it is not a CI test.

[`jev_context.py --check`](../bench/grading/scripts/jev_context.py) verifies that the paired 192-call essay comparison removes only question context, preserves inputs and marking definitions, keeps aggregate all/some/none scoring distinct from item-level partial credit, and records failures without converting them to grades. Live runs require an explicit fresh output directory and a Jev credential; they are opt-in benchmarks, not CI tests.

[`jev_partial_credit.py --check`](../bench/grading/scripts/jev_partial_credit.py) checks explicit/plain scheme isolation, paired context, 0.35/0.65 boundaries, malformed/non-finite decision output, failure accounting and exclusion of unknown gold. Its 144-call-per-mode live comparison asks direct three-way and Boolean decisions together; a first-request format/auth gate stops a bad batch without retrying. Synthetic labels and missing-evidence diagnostics are documented in the grading report; this is not a CI test.

[`jev_contract.py --check`](../bench/grading/scripts/jev_contract.py) validates the three production-contract fixtures against the open-part question shape, checks that the four grading arms send identical question objects and differ only in state or packaging, that no gold label or rationale reaches a model payload, that question text matches the app's `blocksToText` assembly, and covers noul bands, score rounding, malformed responses, failure accounting, false-credit counts and computation-check precision/recall. Live runs (`--experiment grading|compute|latex`) need a fresh output directory and a Jev credential; they are opt-in benchmarks, not CI tests.

[`alt_methods.py --check`](../bench/grading/scripts/alt_methods.py) runs the deterministic alternative-method checker (triangle true-quantity table, coordinate circle figure with theorem preconditions, linear solution-set chain) on every fixture answer and checks that no gold label reaches a Jev payload. Live runs need a Jev credential and a fresh output directory; they are opt-in benchmarks, not CI tests.

### API

| File                                                                      | About                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [`src/api/client.test.ts`](../src/api/client.test.ts)                     | Multipart upload client progress/abort and Huma `llm_credits_exhausted` coded-error parsing. `provider_busy` and its wait are lifted from a Huma envelope. A failed request keeps its trace id on `ApiError.traceId`: the generic answer's `traceId`, else `X-Request-Id`, else the id the request sent (a proxy answered), and a failed upload's too. |
| [`src/api/plateAiTransport.test.ts`](../src/api/plateAiTransport.test.ts) | Scopes Plate AI routes to the workspace and strips browser provider credentials.                                                                                                                                 |
| [`src/api/sse.test.ts`](../src/api/sse.test.ts)                           | Reassembles SSE events that arrive split across response chunks; strips keep-alive comments from event payloads.                                                                                                                                                 |
| [`src/api/chapterReorder.test.ts`](../src/api/chapterReorder.test.ts) | Chapter ordering updates before the server responds, preserves the prior cache for rollback on failure, and invalidates on success or failure. |
| [`src/api/queryPersistence.test.ts`](../src/api/queryPersistence.test.ts) | The stored query cache holds only opted-in successful queries, and an infinite list only its first page. |
| [`src/api/optimisticSettle.test.ts`](../src/api/optimisticSettle.test.ts) | Overlapping optimistic study-preference saves keep the later optimistic value; only the last save to settle invalidates `me`. |
| [`src/api/eventStream.test.ts`](../src/api/eventStream.test.ts) | Runs the events stream effect with real query observers and response streams: fresh authentication and list reconciliation on every connection, `ingest` and `tree` events applied to the file caches, a clean end reconnecting without the banner, a failed connect marking the stream disconnected before backing off, and the reconnect after it retrying a read that failed meanwhile (a first or clean connect does not). |
| [`src/lib/observability.test.ts`](../src/lib/observability.test.ts) | Real Sentry transport checks React render-error capture once, development console logging with component stacks, and filtering API failures owned by the backend. |
| [`src/components/ui/Button.test.tsx`](../src/components/ui/Button.test.tsx) | Checks native/slotted markup parity with both icons inside the styled element, and preserves child classes, link markup and button attributes without icons through the real Radix Slot. |
| [`src/components/banners/SaveBanner.test.tsx`](../src/components/banners/SaveBanner.test.tsx) | The `outdated` save strip renders as one row with its copy and Reload in the close button's place, with no close button. |
| [`src/components/ui/ErrorId.test.tsx`](../src/components/ui/ErrorId.test.tsx) | The error id ("Error ID: …") shows on a route boundary screen, on a failed pane with Retry (`FileError`) and in a `FormAlert` for an unexpected 500, and not for a typed 5xx (`ai_unavailable`); inside the alert the id text is `aria-hidden` and a polite status region carries the copy confirmation. |
| [`src/api/fileListReconcile.test.ts`](../src/api/fileListReconcile.test.ts) | A terminal list status refreshes stale file detail once, without repeatedly downloading completed bodies; a list read keeps client-only ingest progress for a file still ingesting. |
| [`src/lib/errors.test.ts`](../src/lib/errors.test.ts)                     | Normalizes API, network, cancellation, quota, file-cap, credits, ingest-lease, BYOK key, and chunk-load failures into safe UI error kinds and actions; while offline any failure, a chunk-load one included, reads as offline, and an `OfflineError` (a page whose code failed offline) stays offline once the connection is back. A `provider_busy` answer is its own retryable kind. Any snake_case Huma `errors[].message` code parsed by the API client picks its own copy (`revision_conflict` reads as someone else's change with Reload; `workspace_limit_exceeded` is not storage copy) and server text never reaches `errorCopy`. An `account_over_quota` or `storage_quota_exceeded` refusal goes to the open workspace's status once however many surfaces see it (`deferStorageRefusal`), a member's own-quota refusal (numbers present) keeps its surface's copy while the owner's goes to the status, and the own-account frozen copy stays outside a workspace. `unexpectedErrorId` gives the trace id of a 5xx without a typed code (one carrying a one-word code such as `timeout` included) or an unknown status without a code, and none for a typed 5xx, a typed refusal, a 404 or an exception. |
| [`src/lib/appRelease.test.ts`](../src/lib/appRelease.test.ts) | The release nudge: when a visible tab regains focus it reads `/release.txt` uncached, and another release makes the next path change a full page load (not a search change, not while offline, where the first path change back online loads), once per served release per tab across a stale edge; a hidden tab, a second read within a minute, the same release, a 404, the SPA fallback's HTML, a failed read and a build without a release do nothing; a refused client protocol counts as an update without a load loop; the outdated strip's Reload loads only once the release file names another release, and neither while the same app is served nor when the check fails. |
| [`src/lib/online.test.ts`](../src/lib/online.test.ts) | `waitsForConnection`: only a paused query without data shows the waiting state; one holding data, an empty list included, keeps it on screen. |
| [`src/lib/localDb.test.ts`](../src/lib/localDb.test.ts) | Browser-local attempts per quiz newest first, card state stored with each logged review, and one stable anonymous id (fake IndexedDB). |
| [`src/lib/shareLink.test.ts`](../src/lib/shareLink.test.ts) | Signed `/w/` share paths and material tokens match the Go vectors (a material token never verifies as a summary path), verify only under the signing secret, and MSW seed paths stay signed with the dev secret. |
| [`src/lib/googlePicker.test.ts`](../src/lib/googlePicker.test.ts) | Google multi-select, folder selection, origin/app id, and picked/cancel/error callback dispatch. |
| [`src/lib/onedrivePicker.test.ts`](../src/lib/onedrivePicker.test.ts)     | OneDrive mixed file/folder multi-selection with persistence and popup cleanup, picker host from `/me/drive`, personal vs work scopes, `event.origin` allowlist, pick payload to import `fileIds`/`driveIds`, tenant-consent vs user-cancel mapping, and Google Picker env rejection of missing keys and non-numeric project ids. |

| [`src/lib/fileIcons.test.ts`](../src/lib/fileIcons.test.ts)               | Every `FileIcon` name has a symbol in the Catppuccin sprite and vice versa; shared file-tree, recent-item and search icon mapping refines backend `FileKind` by extension (code files, video under audio, csv under sheet, dotless names, filename-only public summaries) with the kind as fallback; material mappings include diagram → api-blueprint. |

Fails if the handover keeps nothing or every change rebuilds every block. A Mermaid block and a table whose children have no ids keep their painted objects at the handover (fails when the comparison counts the random ids normalization gives them, REVIEW5 N1). |

| [`src/features/materials/yTextCodeMirror.test.ts`](../src/features/materials/yTextCodeMirror.test.ts) | Two CodeMirror states bound to one Mermaid source text through 300 rounds of concurrent multi-range edits end equal to each other and to both texts. |
| [`src/features/materials/inertTex.test.ts`](../src/features/materials/inertTex.test.ts) | MathLive's HTML commands (`\style`, `\htmlStyle`, `\class`, `\htmlClass`, `\cssId`, `\htmlId`, `\htmlData`, `\href`, one built by `\csname`, one inside `\text` and one after a `\\` line break) write a style, class, id, data attribute, link or background fetch into MathLive's markup, and none once passed through `inertTex`; `\url` becomes text and ordinary LaTeX is unchanged. The pinned MathLive (>= 0.111) renders `\color`/`\colorbox`/`\bbox`/`\fcolorbox`/`\fontfamily` and every `\enclose` notation's colour, border, background and shadow options with no paint/background fetch and no event handler, so a downgrade fails this test (security regression: read-only formulas draw MathLive's HTML commands as text; the pinned MathLive validates the CSS and SVG values LaTeX takes). |

An open whose card is stale (`opened(true)`) draws and uploads once, a second call in the same open adds nothing, and a fresh open (`opened(false)`) uploads nothing then or later; a stale session read counts once, though a new editor opens on it again (View and back), while a new stale read counts; a stale open the editor may not capture yet goes with the idle upload; a stale open refused as too soon (`429`) with no local edit ends its cycle (nothing at 60 s or on leaving), while one with a local edit sends again. An update from elsewhere (`remote`) after a drawing means leaving sends nothing and the idle upload draws again first; one during a drawing discards it; after a refused upload it keeps the refused drawing from going again on leaving (review round 2 H1b). Each fix of review T3 (S1, S2, the size cap, the headers) fails its test when reverted (checked 2026-10-11); so do the stale open, the remote drop, the mid-draw discard, the refused-upload guard and the refused stale open's end (checked 2026-10-14). |

## Collaboration Rust server (`collaboration-rs/crates/server`, `sources`, `ydoc`)

`pnpm test:collaboration` builds the notes markdown bundle and runs `cargo test` over the workspace in `collaboration-rs/` (the server is a binary crate: `--bins` for its unit tests alone). The integration tests need the docker e2e stack's Postgres, Redis and Go API (see the file headers); locally they skip without their variables. CI's `collaboration` job runs them all against that stack (`--include-ignored`, with `CAPY_IT_REQUIRED` making a missing variable a failure, and the `sources` DB tests failing without theirs), plus `cargo fmt`/`clippy` for the workspace's crates. Security regressions count work (`ydoc::steps`, yrs's own work counter, allocations), never wall-clock time; each was checked failing with its fix reverted (rust-core PROGRESS, security table).

| File | About |
| --- | --- |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/auth.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/auth.rs) | Room names parse and print; a minted HS256 token verifies, and wrong rooms, expiry, access and signatures are refused; the service secret compares whole (security regression: collaboration token room binding and expiry); the client protocol minimum goes by the room family (material and keys rooms, source rooms). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/model.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/model.rs) | A note stored with a quiz reference still carrying an imported fence body is never served: its room refuses to load, as Go's reads refuse it (security regression: live-items REVIEW5 pending references reaching the room's readers; fails if the room loads it); a stored Plate document the store would refuse loads read-only whatever the reason (a type the catalog does not list, one it lists for another material kind, a closed element's extra key, a reference without its row), its reason kept for the load to log, while a valid note and mindmap load writable; a stored quiz holding a question block kind its rooms do not admit loads its room read-only (Epo 2026-10-13). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/projection.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/projection.rs) | A quiz holding a block this version cannot read projects to nothing (the projection would drop the block), and its refusal is held per stored version: new once (logged once), held until the version moves, so the pending scan stops retrying it every 5 s (review R2-L2; fails if the refusal is not held). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/edits.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/edits.rs) | A server write of a quiz whose documents hold a block kind its rooms do not admit is refused, so no rebuilt quiz drops the block (review M1); a note edit whose result the whole-note check refuses (an 11th interactive block across the note) is an `invalid_input` refusal naming the rule, not an unknown outcome; a text source's ambiguous span keeps its lines through the sources error into the refusal body. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/note_store.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/note_store.rs) | A quiz's lineage rebuild keeps its incomplete questions and their keys (Epo 2026-10-14): an mcq with no correct option, a blank part text and a blank worked solution, typed into the log after the base, read back the same after the rebuild while the projection drops the question (fails if compaction rebuilds from the projection or loses a blank block or a key). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/protocol.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/protocol.rs) | capy-sync frames round-trip several messages, malformed frames are refused, a JOIN carries the client protocol after the token (none from a client before the gate), a protocol below the family minimum or above the server's is outdated, awareness payloads decode, and a frame carries at most 64 messages (security regression: rust-core REVIEW1 S5). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/markers.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/markers.rs) | Contributor markers are written once per store window, stay live after a failed store, and client updates may not touch them. A marker names no user: the room's state holds neither the writer's user id nor its base64 (the old key), only the room maps the opaque key back, and a marker it did not draw is refused as naming no writer (Epo 2026-10-12: readers receive the markers). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/access.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/access.rs) | Read-only narrowing and the refusal reasons the editor acts on. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/http.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/http.rs) (`a_room_being_reset_refuses_edits_without_a_report`) | A room being reset refuses an agent edit (`/internal/documents/edit`) and the replace-block command (`/internal/commands`) before anything applies: a 503 whose outcome is known (`unavailable_target`, for both) and no Sentry event, since the caller may retry. The requests run through the router on an app whose database and Redis are never reached. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/http.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/http.rs) (`sockets_read_through_an_8_kib_buffer`) | The WebSocket upgrade sets the 8 KiB read buffer (`READ_BUFFER`), so an idle connection stays near the runbook's 30 KiB (REVIEW5 final N4). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/main.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/main.rs) | A graceful stop deletes the source disk store's files once the rooms stored: names the store writes (`base-<sha>`, `tmp-3`) go and others (`notes.txt`) stay (Epo 2026-10-17; fails with the clear removed from `shutdown`). Linux only: with the soft limit first lowered to 1024 (as a container starts it), the startup raise leaves `/proc/self/limits` showing the open-file soft limit equal to the hard one (prod capacity run: a container's soft 1024 held the server near 1,000 connections). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/config.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/config.rs) | Local-dev values, origins and overrides, the UAT publication hold only for exactly `true`, the Office engine pool and the source disk store (folder and cap) required with no default, bad numbers refused. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/health.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/health.rs) | `collab_health` percentiles and store timings that reset each interval. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/incidents.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/incidents.rs) | Edit incident reason tokens, broadcast causes, client payloads without the incident, and `step2_unplaced` recorded once until placed (the wiring of both is in `rooms.rs`). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/app.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/app.rs), [`source_rooms.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/source_rooms.rs) | A socket's backlog past max(4 MiB, the largest frame it was sent) closes the reader (security regression: rust-core REVIEW1 D2); the handoff's room commands a full queue held back keep their order. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/observe.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/observe.rs) | Log time shape, Sentry event id validation, and a reported error not sent again. Every capture carries `service: collaboration`; an internal request's captures carry its W3C `traceparent` trace id (none for an all-zero or malformed one), through the router's `request_trace` layer too (`http.rs` test). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/pubsub.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/src/pubsub.rs) | Eviction acknowledgements complete once every expected instance answered, a refusal fails the request, and an eviction id runs once. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/server/tests/socket_buffers.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/server/tests/socket_buffers.rs) | The vendored tungstenite (`collaboration-rs/third_party/tungstenite`): after a socket sends and flushes a 2 MiB message, its write buffer's capacity is back under 64 KiB, and a small message after it still goes out; without the patch the socket keeps 2,097,162 bytes (Epo 2026-10-11: a live View viewer of a 2 MB note kept about 2.3 MiB). After it reads a 2 MiB frame and 300 small ones, its read buffer is back under 64 KiB; without the patch it keeps 2,073,550 bytes (REVIEW5 final S3: any joined peer could pin up to the frame cap per socket). Counted, not timed. |

upgrade origin); every internal route answers 401 without the service secret or with a wrong one (security regression: internal routes' service secret); a panic in an update, inside a transaction (whose editors hear the rejection without its incident) or during a room's load costs neither the process nor later joins (security regression: rust-core REVIEW1 S2, notes REVIEW2 panic = unwind, edit-incidents REVIEW2 m-B); ## Collaboration Rust notes crate (`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/`)

Parity tests against a corpus the Node collaboration server wrote (`tests/corpus/*.json.gz`, regenerated with `tests/corpus/generate.sh <checkout> [families]` from a checkout from before the Rust cutover, whose `collaboration/src` is the oracle). Run with `cargo test --release -p notes` in `collaboration-rs/`.

| File | About |
| ---- | ----- |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/projection.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/projection.rs) | The Plate JSON projection of corpus notes (the near-limit load-test note, e2e and bench fixtures, marks, tables, voids, embeds) equals slate-yjs's, through `project` and `project_state`, and `slate_yjs::state_from_value` bootstraps a note whose projection is the value it was given. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/ops.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/ops.rs) | Random Slate operation sequences recorded through slate-yjs (15 corpus files: seed notes, the load note, an over-limit note, and small notes with marks, nesting, tables and voids) replay through the crate's editor and slate-yjs mapping: each step's Plate JSON, metrics and outcome equal Node's, and how many updates carry the same items is reported. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/bound.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/bound.rs) | Three writers on stale copies delivering merged or queued updates (`multi-1`..`multi-6`): every update's growth bound equals the JS bound, refusals and deferrals match, and the room's metrics and Plate JSON equal Node's; a delivery Node took and the key rule refuses (the corpus's writers insert blocks without ids) is counted and applied, and the validator re-measures. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/fuzz_admit.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/fuzz_admit.rs) | Deterministic mutation fuzz of the room path: 2,000 real updates (typing, deletes, a format, an attribute, new and removed blocks, a paste, three concurrent writers, items each naming a unit of a paragraph as their right origin, 40 chained clients, and items splitting every held item of a paragraph) with one to six bytes mutated (fixed xorshift seed) are decoded, admitted and, when admitted, applied, measured and projected; it fails on a panic, on admission walks over 4 steps per byte of the update and the room (counted, not timed), or on a case with structs counting no step, and prints the case. `FUZZ_CASES` and `FUZZ_SEED` run more. |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/yjs_traffic.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/yjs_traffic.rs) | Traffic made by Yjs 13.6.31 itself (`corpus/yjs-traffic.cjs`: 60 seeds of 2-6 docs editing nested blocks, delivered as reconnect diffs, draft-run, overlapping and cross-doc `Y.mergeUpdates` bundles, 3,641 deliveries with fixed client ids) is admitted or unheld, never refused, except deliveries the key rule refuses because the generator inserts blocks without an id and sets their type in a later transaction (counted; the note editor writes an element with its type and id in one); so are the six deliveries the round 8 placement replay refused or miscounted (`corpus/yjs-review4-pairs.json`). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/admit_cost.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/admit_cost.rs) | `Validator::admit` stays linear on every update shape the reviews built to find quadratic paths, against a room whose validator holds a bound, counted in the steps of admission's walks (`ydoc::steps`, per walk: at most 4 per byte of the update and the room in all, and for an admitted update at least one per struct in each of the struct pass, the container walk, the content check and the placement, so a stubbed counter or one walk's missing step fails it): a 3,000-block paste, 10,000 chained clients and 400 origin-less items over 2,000 tombstones, taken (security regression: notes REVIEW2 M5, REVIEW3 M8), a 40,000-item forward chain across two clients (a cycle), a 20,000-level block chain, 40,000 prepended characters, a class of 100 typing into one empty paragraph (taken); 20,000 origin-less items over 20,000 tombstones from one client or forged ones, 10,000 fanned items, 20,000 chained clients and 40,000 splits of a held run are refused as too costly on the trial copy, as are the fanned, chained and split shapes up to the 4 MiB message cap (security regression: notes REVIEW4 M9-M11, Epo work budget); the reviewer's forward chain in one client is malformed. A writer's 8-byte update deleting all 32,000 plain attributes of one paragraph stays inline (`heavy` false) and goes in within 4 steps per byte for `heavy` and admission alike, with no entry lookup (`Walk::Entries`) for keys no element requires; the checks read an element's entries only through that counted lookup, and the test refuses a scan of an element's map in `limits.rs`, which the steps could not count (the M3 scan took 89 s in debug) (security regression: REVIEW5 M3, seconds of key checks on an async worker). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/bound_cost.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/bound_cost.rs) | Selecting and deleting every paragraph of a heavily formatted note prices zero growth in at least one step per deleted range, and 4,000 paragraphs take at most five times the bound's walk steps of 1,000, the delete-set lookups' probes among them (a quadratic bound, or a lookup scanning the set, takes sixteen; security regression: plate REVIEW1 M1 in Rust). |
| [`collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/validator.rs`](../collaboration-rs/crates/notes/tests/validator.rs) | The room validator's cases ported from `limits.test.ts` (unsupported roots refused also between measurements, growth past a limit refused while an over-limit note stays shrinkable, deep growth behind shrinking content, an unplaceable update deferred without a copy, typing near the size limit with one copy, runtime comment marks measured out of text leaves) and hostile updates on a 2 MiB thread: a block chain at, one past and 20,000 levels past the 1,024-level ceiling (refused, measured and projected without running out of stack; security regression: notes REVIEW1 M1), a 10,000-level attribute value (a decode error; security regression: notes REVIEW1 M2), the 6-byte update listing a client without structs (taken as nothing on the exact and the bound path; security regression: notes REVIEW1 M3), and binary text content, a buffer or a shared type as an attribute (refused) while a mark and a plain attribute pass (security regression: notes REVIEW1 D2). The legacy `image` type is refused as a type the embed catalog does not list. An image, audio or file element without its asset id, with an empty one, or with a `url` or `src` is refused when the update arrives, whether the update brings the element, sets the URL on an image, makes a paragraph an image or deletes the asset id; one naming its asset, or replacing it, goes in (Epo 2026-10-10; security regression: REVIEW5 N1, an update the store would refuse and so discard the room). A `src` set on a held paragraph takes no copy of the room and stays inline, while a URL set on an image is checked on one copy off the async workers (`limits::heavy`; security regression: REVIEW5 M1, a room copy per 16-byte update on a tokio worker). An update that leaves an element without a key the store requires is refused at admission with no copy: a deleted `type`, a deleted top-level `id`, a new top-level block without an id or a type, a paragraph made a reference without its material id, a reference's material id deleted; a deleted optional key, a new block with its keys, a retyped heading and deleted whole blocks go in (security regression: REVIEW5 R2, an update the store refuses discarded the room for every editor). A writer that already wrote pastes several blocks and an image, and it goes in (the checks no longer ask yrs for a clock the room has not seen, which panicked) (security regression: REVIEW5 recheck, a yrs index panic in the key and media checks). A `type` overwritten with the new entry deleted in the same update (a writer's two updates merged), and a stale lower client's losing `type` sent with a delete of the current one, are refused on the bound and exact paths; the losing write alone goes in, read on the trial copy such an update gets, with no copy of its own (security regression: REVIEW5 R2b, a block left without its type discarded the room). The reviewer's 21-byte crafted small edit, two `type` entries after one, is inline by size yet `heavy` (its key check reads the result), goes in and takes one key copy (security regression: REVIEW5 final S2, that copy on an async worker, repeatably); with a refusal beside it (25 bytes), `heavy` and admit agree on 100 runs: refused inline, no copy (security regression: REVIEW5 final S1, the check stopped in a random order at the first element needing a copy).A new reference carrying an imported fence body (`pending`), one set on a held reference and a new reference without its material id are refused at admit, as the store check refuses them (security regression: live-items REVIEW5 pending references; fails without the `pending` refusal or with an empty material id holding). A reference created or retyped below the top level (a quoted reference, a quote line retyped) is refused at admit as the store refuses it, while a top-level one goes in (live-items REVIEW5 R2; fails without the check). |

Cases the Rust store refuses by the rules it took from Go and the browser at the cutover (card caps and image, media assets, no diagram/mindmap node types) or from the embed catalog (a type or material kind it does not list, an element in a kind it does not list it for, Go's "chart, plot and graph" wording) are counted apart from the comparison, as are a `multi` answer, merged into `mcq` since Epo 2026-10-15 (`holds_multi`), and the cases the Node server refused for a figure without a description, optional since Epo 2026-10-14 (`loosened`). The every-block note fixture is valid once its imported fences name their rows; a reference keeping an imported fence body, which the Node validator accepted, is counted among the cutover's tightenings; a card front holds at most 4,000 characters and a back 2,000, counted as Go counts runes (astral characters once), and a card image is exactly `{assetId}` with a non-blank id of at most 128 (security regression: a stored set Go cannot project breaks the material for everyone); image and audio blocks need a non-empty `assetId` and no `url`/`src`; diagram and mindmap materials need a `mermaid` block. A plot block takes Go's `validatePlot` bounds (one to four curves of 2 to 60 `[x, y]` points, at most eight reference lines and labelled points, labels and titles as strings): a valid plot goes in alone, in a quiz question's stem and as a note's embed, and Go's refused cases (five curves, 61 points, nine marks, one point, three numbers, a text coordinate, a `z` axis, an unlabelled mark, a missing y title, an unknown key) are refused (main 053860dd, Epo 2026-10-12: plots in Rust only). An image's or graph's description holds `QUESTION_DESCRIPTION_MAX` (500) in a quiz question and a note graph, refused at 501, and twice that in a bank question (Epo 2026-10-14). |

room admission refuses a block created or retyped to a type the catalog does not list for a Plate document, `OWN_KEYS` equals the catalog's required keys, and each element of the note losing a required key or gaining a forbidden one (a video's `assetId`, a Mermaid's `code`) is refused, a nested Mermaid's id among them (decision 1; each check reverted fails it); the one shared-text key is the server's and the seed makes it a text on that element alone; one media list (the elements requiring `assetId`, plus the video for what an edit may not insert); a `type` write that loses Yjs's conflict order cannot slip an extra key onto a plot (review M3); a figure or interactive block below the top level, an eleventh interactive block, and the values the store checks (a reference's kind, a video's provider and id, a width, an interactive block's html size, a caption that is not a string or is one character past `BLOCK_CAPTION_MAX`, a figure's block, a Mermaid source) are refused while valid ones go in, a 300-character caption among them (review M4) (each check reverted fails it). |

The ported Node tests of main's agent-edit changes: links and inline equations survive an edit beside a replaced span that starts or ends at one; a missed target carries the block as it reads now, a gone id the first 60 blocks with 80-unit previews and the total (a preview cut inside an emoji keeps it whole, 81 units, where Node keeps half); an insert whose anchor is gone lands at the end, or after the nearest surviving block when the same call removed or replaced the anchor, lists the relocation and undoes; inspect shows inline and display math as `$tex$`/`$$tex$$`, a `replace_text` span over math is refused with the current text, and `replace_block` rewrites a block in place, refuses a stale expectation, a taken id or a media block, and undoes back to the original. An edit inserts a library figure, an `img` naming its asset with no URL, at the top level or inside a block; a URL image, an image with an empty asset id, an audio block and an asset id on a paragraph are refused as media (main 142ca296: Go stores the figure as the note's asset before the edit). The Undo of an edit that inserted a figure removes it again (`undo_material_commands`), while an edit itself is still refused removing a block holding media (agent-writes: Undo of a figure insert was refused). Every refused `replace_text` span carries its block, an expected text occurring twice included, and a stale `set_property` (`set_mermaid`) the value it holds (`ambiguous_spans_and_stale_properties_carry_the_current_target`); the corpus comparison leaves that block out of the ambiguous and empty expected texts Node refused without one (2026-10-14); an inspection lists its blocks as a gone id's refusal does (`edit::listing`); a text source's expected text occurring more than once lists the lines its occurrences start in (UTF-16 `start`, each line once, at most 20, its text cut after 1,000 characters with a trailing `…`; a 25,000-character line holding 5,000 occurrences is listed once, cut) and one not found carries nothing (`an_ambiguous_text_source_span_lists_the_lines_it_occurs_in`). |

Needs the bundle from `node markdown/build.mjs` (or `NOTES_MARKDOWN_BUNDLE`), built before the crate's tests run in CI. A source whose conversion changed on purpose since the freeze (footnotes become text) is checked on its own instead (`changed`). |

For a workspace note and a workspace quiz: the asset records the workspace and the material; a save trashes ready assets it no longer references once they completed over a minute ago (pending and fresh ones stay; the charge stays), a save using one again restores it, the sweep deletes one trashed over a day ago and releases its bytes, trashing the material keeps them, a workspace clone carries them to the cloned material, and purging deletes them. A standalone note clone gives the note's image to the cloned note and its embedded quiz's image to the cloned quiz. Adopt returns the target's own asset unchanged (restored from the trash), copies a readable ready asset (a trashed one too) (same object path, blob refcount 2, target payer charged), omits pending, deleted, unknown and unreadable ones (a link-shared standalone note's become adoptable) and one over the payer's cap for its purpose (an image over the image cap, audio over the per-file cap), refuses a viewer and over the quota makes no copy and says `refused`. `TestEditorAssetMaterialTakesPayerCaps`: a material's caps follow its payer's plan, images 2 MiB Free and 5 MiB Pro, audio 10 MiB Free and 30 MiB Pro (security regression: human backend-storage-quota 2026-10-09 plan image cap). |

A copy of bytes an export-only publication left unindexed keeps its due `reprocess_at`. A note's index travels only while current: edited since (dirty), its copy in a single and a workspace clone starts dirty with no index. Pin mismatch: the copy is queued for ingest (pending, unindexed, the cloner's reservation, a processed store-only Office file in fast mode), its bytes stay charged to the payer, and an exhausted cloner is refused (`CreditsExhaustedError`). A file past the payer's per-file plan cap is refused: a Pro editor's clone into a Free owner's workspace hits the Free cap while the same clone into the editor's own workspace lands, and a workspace clone is refused as a whole (`FileTooLargeError`). Explore's `canClone` follows the clone rule: a public viewer false, a member or public editor true, a standalone public item true. Two concurrent clones into a workspace one file below its cap leave one copy and one file-limit refusal; with room, both land under one new chapter. A file whose saves since its last fold are still rows (migration 0075) clones with those rows as epoch 1: the copy opens on the same document, its `storage_bytes` and log rows equal the source's, and the payer is charged the file plus its document, as reconciliation recounts. `TestClonesCopyPartialFiles` (parts design Q2): a workspace clone and a file clone into another workspace copy a summary-partial and a page-partial file as `failed` and indexed, served by ready content of the copied `content_hash` with its vector, the missing summary row still missing and the NULL `pipeline_identity` still NULL; Retry processing by the copy's owner queues a `partsOnly` ingest and a parse job with `parseFee` false; a failed unindexed file is not copied (workspace clone) or not found (file clone). |

| [`pipeline/tests/test_agent.py`](../pipeline/tests/test_agent.py)                             | Chat agent streaming, search-hit locations usable by scoped document reads with stable citation numbers and next-start paging, one `search_workspace` per response with an overlap footer when a search mostly re-finds shown passages, per-search telemetry events with answer-cited hits attributed at turn end (a failed write never fails the turn), atomic file/chapter tool scope, scoped material provenance, citations, retries, checkpoints, and disconnect handling, and clamping model-authored material titles to `MATERIAL_TITLE_MAX` before the gateway call. Program answers: an OpenUI Lang program streams through unchanged with live citation events in statement order and a final event in reading order carrying each entry's `n`; incomplete and invalid programs retain their text and citations for local browser recovery, plain prose uses Markdown, and an empty response before the turn's first write fails with `invalid_answer`, all without an extra model request. Fenced programs are unwrapped; captured images ride into the next request only, after their tool results and count by image patches in the context budget. Also the agent-tool contract (operations table, schema validation), deterministic operation ids, trash tools narrowing a restricted scope, the unified source/material listing with editability and material kinds (non-note kinds pointing at `inspect_document` and `edit_document`) and restricted or empty source scopes, and inspect/edit document tools rendering blocks (an embedded material reference with its id, or flagged as not yet created), lines and Office entries and refreshing the turn's evidence after an edit. Completed turns replay contract-selected full listings and only cited read/search chunks without another tool call. Library evidence: only excerpt pages used in successful writes are retained, replay is deduplicated, a retained page lets a follow-up edit write only while its current full text is in context (changed, missing or compacted text refuses the edit), and a turn with Library off replays none. A material written from the library sends its resolved provenance and needs no indexed workspace scope, two materials may name the same excerpts, a library `edit_document` naming no excerpts sends the provenance of the reads since the last write, and an `edit_document` on a source file takes no excerpt ids, sends no provenance and credits no read. With Library on, a library that is down or holds no subject leaves the turn on the workspace without the library tools, the failure logged; with the flag off the same library leaves the turn on the workspace. A retained excerpt read stores its `count` and replays with it. The turn context is sent last and left in place so every request extends the previous one, showing `Open: nothing.`, then the excerpts read and the planning countdown, then the build budget once a write lands. The budget: reads and a refused write never lift the planning cap; a 20-response turn landing a write every third response runs to the end with no response ceiling; once a write lands the stall guard turns tools off after five responses without another (the tools still sent, the turn context's final notice saying they are off; calls a tools-off response makes anyway are not run, answered with a not-run result and asked once more, and a second such response ends the turn) and reports `stall` with the writes landed logged; each of the first two errored writes extends that threshold by two (nine barren responses after three refused writes); an empty response after a write counts as one barren response with the loop carrying on to the answer, while five consecutive empties turn tools off and the sixth ends the turn on the guard; a credit-exhausted terminal call with no text reports `planning_cap` instead of the guard; four of seven tool calls in one response are accepted and the rest refused; and the 160-call cap (patched down) refuses the excess call and runs one tools-off response carrying the final notice, reported as `tool_cap`. A retained excerpt enters already credited, so a write naming none credits it only when nothing new was read. A turn before its first write counts its responses down in the turn context until the tools-off one, and a tools-off response that writes a tool call as text ends the turn at `planning_cap` with no answer and no `response_flagged` error. `list_sources` lists a material under the chapter it is filed in (the header carries `chapter_id`) and the rest under unfiled study materials. A write naming no excerpts credits only the read books whose read text shares a word 4-gram or an example number with it that no other read book holds (what the read tools add, such as head lines, chunk prefixes and links, and a slide's SVG markup ignored), every candidate when nothing matches, and reads provenance once, while named excerpts are credited as before; carried and retained reads keep their text for it. Blank optional arguments (null, `""`, `"null"`, `[]`, `{}`) are left out before checking, into an edit command's variant, with required ones kept as sent and the handler receiving the cleaned arguments. Edit refusals: an argument error names the command's missing, unknown or invalid fields without echoing it; a missed edit target comes back as it reads now (the block list for a gone id; an expected text occurring twice too) for the model only, saying when it changed since the turn last inspected or edited it; an edit receipt says where an insert whose anchor was gone landed; and a write naming unread excerpts credits them with no refusal; a receipt names the note blocks the write added and leaves out the `"end"` anchor's relocation; an AskUser tool call ending the turn on its question (prose as Md, choices from JSON text) with nothing run, every AskUser call of a response becoming a question up to three; the notice under the results read by the last response with tools, asking to write when the editing skill is in the request and to answer otherwise, and the countdown and final notice worded the same way; create_material refusing an unknown chapter before the gateway; an Office edit receipt's note on the characters its text lost reaching the tool result |

| [`pipeline/tests/test_ingest_worker.py`](../pipeline/tests/test_ingest_worker.py)             | Validated local parse-to-ingest handoff, bundle deletion once no other active job names it, optional cache-registration cleanup for a reaped object, one-time corrupt-artifact repair, parser OOM and Office format-mismatch answers failing without a retry, post-parse retries, the donor key unchanged across releases, processing-plan execution, the chunk stage applying the bundle's frozen furniture and preferring `parsed.pdf`, then the Office preview, then the spooled source as the page model (a bundle without `refinement.json` is terminal), parser capacity waits, source reuse with optional donor-cache `HEAD` failures treated as misses, exact-claim heartbeat cancellation, source-fenced terminal reaping, admission-refusal identity fields and its failed event carrying the `indexed` `_finish_fail` wrote, the terminal failed event of the worker and of the lease reaper's announcement carrying the written `indexed`, accounting errors (a final receipt failure, missing model capacity) without job requeue, replacement fencing, content identity including extraction confidence and reasons, and required Office preview byte/type validation, bad-object replacement, and ready-state enforcement. A busy provider re-pends the ingest job once without spending an attempt, then fails the file as `provider_busy`. A source refresh of a parsed file records its parser pages at zero credits, while an upload and a file's first parse through a refresh pay the page fee; a system-paid reprocess of an export-only file records them at zero credits too, and so does Retry processing of a partial file whose payload says `parseFee: false`. A CSV over the projection token cap is refused while its rows are projected, before chunking or any embedding call, and a table chunks with no overlap whatever the configured overlap (no row repeats; the chunk count equals the no-overlap packing). A file over a documented limit (`InputLimitError`) fails its job with the limit's error code and no Sentry event, and ends ready and unindexed with that code as its index limit and a ready, unindexed progress event (an edited source over it records no limit); other terminal failures are captured and fail the file. A refused Office rebase (`office_rebase_refused`) sends the workspace no failed progress event; any other terminal failure does. A source refresh publishes no progress or failure event, only its terminal ready event. A note index payload (`materialId`, no file or revision) passes the heartbeat on its owner's lifecycle (an owner pending deletion cancels it), requeues a retryable failure instead of being cancelled, is admitted at the provider boundary and the claim fence only for its own note (a changed `materialId` is a lost claim), re-pends a busy provider once without spending an attempt or touching a file and then fails, and abandons its claimed content row when indexing fails. The card thumbnail takes the `page1.png` extraction wrote, with no second copy of the bundle. The bundle's `vlm/<page>.jpg` renders are read through the caption cache without its lock (`exclusive=False`); each read page becomes one `vlm-page` text block after that page's own blocks with the whole-page box `[0, 0, 1000, 1000]`, and a page that comes back empty is reported unread (1-based). Bound as a source refresh, the same read goes through the caption cache instead of failing as superseded: its association is inserted unpublished, nothing is promoted, and the digest goes on the candidate. The descriptor is a part (parts design tests 2 and 9): a busy summary provider publishes the content and re-pends without finishing the file, the re-claim reuses the published content and only fills the descriptor (busy again, the file finishes with the summary missing, else complete), and any other summary failure finishes the file with the summary missing; a busy VLM page re-pends the job, and once the job's one re-pend is spent (`provider_waits` reaching the page reads as `last_wait`) a page still busy is left unread so the file ends partial (parts design §6); a donor with a pending descriptor is filled inline by an ingest worker, while a parse coordinator hands off a parts-only continuation (`partsOnly`, no bundle, no local source) instead of finishing. A donor copy publishes before the file becomes ready. An empty parse (with or without unread pages) fails the file before anything is attached; a `partsOnly` continuation of a PDF skips the parse-handoff check and reaches the fill with no "queued" progress first; `_finish_fail` drops a limited file's alias before setting it ready and sets `indexed` from what the alias serves. |

A failed retry stays `indexed` while its alias serves (the lease reaper's failure too, whose reclaimed row reports it) and a first failed ingest does not, and `_finish_fail` returns the `indexed` it wrote; account deletion's `cancel_user_async_work` (migration 0087) keeps a served file indexed and a first ingest unindexed, also one whose alias points at its own processing content; a file over a limit loses its alias and previous content and ends ready, unindexed, with its `index_limit`. A waiter re-attaches once the creator publishes, so its alias moves off its old index; publish refuses a superseded file revision and leaves the content processing; `_finish_ok` refuses a file whose alias moved; attaching content that is already ready moves a served alias at once. |

## Playwright app copy (`e2e/i18n/`)

Every Playwright config (e2e, UAT, editor, bench/editor) imports `e2e/i18n/compile.ts`, which compiles `messages/` into the ignored `e2e/.paraglide` when it is older than `messages/` (built in a temporary folder and swapped in, so parallel runs never read a half-written folder); `pnpm test:uat:verifier` runs it first too. Specs import `m` from `e2e/i18n` and locate app copy with `m.*`; state assertions read component data attributes (`data-save-state`, `data-sort`/`data-order`, `data-resend`, `data-result`, `data-block-type`, `data-block-style`, `data-privacy`, `data-share-role`, `data-unread-count`, `data-width`, `data-slide`, the save banner's `data-kind`, `ErrorState`'s `data-error-kind` read by `expectErrorSurface`) instead of displayed text. Seed data, typed text, MathLive and BetterOffice labels stay literal.

## Playwright e2e — real stack (`e2e/errors/`, `e2e/sharing/`, `e2e/study/`)

Real stack via Docker (`pnpm e2e`). Editor specs are ignored by the root Playwright config. CI runs it as
its own job (`e2e_stack`) and prebuilds the collaboration and server images
with the GitHub Actions layer cache, so `E2E_PREBUILT_IMAGES=true` starts the
stack with `--no-build`; locally the setup still runs `up --build`.

E2E tests check functional outcomes and may select individual UI elements. They
do not enforce UI layout, DOM wrapper structure, or section/menu organization.
Authorization rules (who gets 401, 404 or 403) are asserted in Go
(`server/internal/httpapi/share_access_test.go` and its neighbours); these
browser suites cover what users see.

Shared upload inputs belong in [`e2e/fixtures/files/`](../e2e/fixtures/files/README.md).
Its guide describes scenario sets, format-specific edits, and expected persisted
results. The heavy UAT lifecycle runner explicitly selects its fixture cases; adding
files alone does not execute them. New local E2E coverage should prioritize upload and
processing failures, with provider failures injected in the isolated test stack.

The four local workers open up to eight Chromium contexts against one Vite dev
server and the Dockerized API. On a machine that cannot absorb that, API
responses stretch to several seconds and the editor takes tens of seconds to
reach `Synced`, so budgets calibrated for a healthy host expire — the collab and
material-mode specs fail even though the flows complete. Use `pnpm e2e:slow`
(one worker) there; it is a host-capacity workaround, not a fix for a flaky spec.

## Playwright e2e — authorized UAT (`e2e/uat/`)

Opt-in: a **Deploy UAT** run only probes service health. These run on a manual
**Deterministic UAT quality** dispatch and as the production promotion gate.

Remote Chromium against an explicitly authorized UAT deployment
(`pnpm e2e:uat`). It requires the dedicated Clerk UAT keys, five synthetic
account emails, and private fixture IDs described in
[`deployment-runbook.md`](deployment-runbook.md#12-uat-review-environment-and-external-service-sandboxes).
It never starts or targets the local E2E auth-bypass stack.
The authorization configuration excludes `journeys/`. The separate manual
critical-path suite uploads through the UAT server and uses the shared
nonproduction ingest project. See the [journey setup and cleanup guide](../e2e/uat/journeys/README.md).

| File                                                                | About                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| [`e2e/uat/authorization.spec.ts`](../e2e/uat/authorization.spec.ts) | Short-lived Clerk Backend API sign-in tokens create real sessions that verify private-workspace visibility, non-disclosing unrelated-user denial, owner/editor workspace statistics, and collaboration-token modes across accounts (owner and editor write, viewer read for live View, unrelated account 404). |

Supporting configuration: [`e2e/uat/playwright.uat.config.ts`](../e2e/uat/playwright.uat.config.ts)
fails closed unless authorization is exactly `true`, all UAT hosts are explicitly
allowed, URL schemes are secure, and every Clerk key, synthetic identity, and
fixture ID is present. CI retains trace, screenshot, video, and HTML evidence
only for the configured artifact window.

The heavy suite (`pnpm e2e:uat:journeys`) has twenty-eight cases (plus one fixme, one manual journey and one Office load measurement outside the lanes)
in lanes (Playwright projects `office`, `app` and `billing`, plus `refresh` for the planned
refresh journeys) that the workflow runs as parallel jobs, each with its own run
id, one worker and no whole-journey retries. Normal CI and lightweight deploy checks exclude it;
manual quality defaults it on and production promotion requires it. Assertions
use backend/storage/provider outcomes, with browser controls used for actions.
Only the Office load measurement records traces (with screenshots); no journey
records video. Sanitized manifests and cleanup reports are retained for 30 days.

| `e2e/uat/journeys/credits.spec.ts` | Purchased credits on four owners, billing lane. The auto-reload card comes through Stripe's hosted setup Checkout (`completeCheckout` in `credits.ts`, test cards 4242 and 0341); payment Checkout is the manual journey's. What Stripe and the reload worker change is asserted on the open tab without reloading it (the stream's `billing` event). Usage's allowance-then-starter hint, purchased marker and Top up link; $7/$101 refused by the field and the API (422); a limit below one $8 top-up refusing it before any Checkout (409, toast, no Stripe customer) and $0 removing it; the setup card shown in the reopened dialog; auto-reload at the balance paying $10 on it (purchased balance on Usage without a page reload, re-armed above the threshold); a limit below this month's spend plus the reload skipping it with no charge, the notice and `credit-reload-skipped` mail (inbox subject from the en template); Chinese chosen in Settings on Usage and Subscription and the next skip mailed with the zh subject; a half refund of the reload taking half its credits back. Declines: a 0341 reload first recorded `retrying` (never `failed` first) with Stripe's `next_payment_attempt` once `invoice.payment_failed` is processed, the retry-date notice and row with Pay, no second reload while Stripe retries; an invoice marked uncollectible through the API is Failed with Pay and paying it with `pm_card_createDispute` credits it (`q3-uncollectible-reload`), then the dispute takes the credits back by webhook, or, when it ran first, is recorded and checked as left to the daily reconciliation; the next decline voided by a new card (shown in the reopened dialog), whose re-armed reload is paid. At zero (blocked until editor commands on non-GLM models settle): about 1,000 credits spent through editor AI commands on the costliest editor model (fails early past 45 calls), the counter and the usage rows agree, a command at zero is refused, the command right after turning auto-reload on at threshold 0 is admitted while its reload declines, one 65 s later is refused with no second reload, switching off voids it and switching on answers `armed: false` with the top-up toast and no reload or window, and a new card re-arms it so its reload is paid and work resumes. Arming on Pro: saves every 250 ms through a reload's charge leave one reload and auto-reload disarmed, and a paid renewal on a test clock re-arms it (a second reload). Every Stripe event relied on is found by object, delivered (`pending_webhooks` 0) and processed without error in `webhook_events`; invoice rows are found by the app's list order. Relies on the labels Top up, Set a limit, Change limit, Auto-reload, Edit, Add a card, Change, Reload amount, When credits reach, Limit per month, Save, Cancel, Language. Needs SELECT on `credit_purchases` for the verifier role. |

Supporting helper (not a test): [`e2e/uat/support.ts`](../e2e/uat/support.ts)
creates short-lived synthetic Clerk sessions and authenticated API calls shared
by the authorization and UI-quality suites. Sign-in fails at once, naming the
asset, when UAT answers an `/assets/*` request with HTML (a site deploy still
propagating, or the edge caching the SPA fallback under a chunk's name).

The rich-content DOCX journey waits for the collaborator to render the owner's
edit and for the target's text cursor before clicking and typing, since remote
layout updates briefly gate hit testing while the accessibility mirror remains.
Failed rich-content convergence retains the last saved text, checkpoint and
epoch in the run's sanitized evidence instead of relying on truncated ZIP bytes.

DOCX preservation checks all six Japanese language metadata entries. The rich
PPTX journey pastes through the real clipboard into the native text input and
checks the exported text. Native tests cover composition; the live journey
does not drive an operating-system IME or provide a slide-text accessibility
mirror. The [September 27 investigation](../artifacts/2026-09-25-office-progress/investigation-2026-09-27.md)
records the original failures, and the [Astra review](../artifacts/2026-09-25-office-progress/review-astra-xhigh-2026-09-27.md)
records the fixes and their verification.
